<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_019hHig2qNZ2nUhB8ZTe1U33`</summary>

````text
1	# Depth Profiling: Code Supplement
2	
3	Code for reproducing the experiments in "Depth Profiling: Classifying Recursive Generation Depth in Base LLM Text Chains."
4	
5	## Requirements
6	
7	- Python 3.10+
8	- CUDA-capable GPU (for generation, feature extraction, and end-to-end classifier)
9	- A reference language model (e.g., EleutherAI/pythia-410m) available locally or via HuggingFace Hub
10	
11	Install dependencies:
12	
13	```bash
14	pip install -r requirements.txt
15	```
16	
17	## Directory Structure
18	
19	`code/` and `data/` are sibling directories under the supplement root. All scripts assume they are run from the `code/` directory and access data via `../data/`.
20	
21	```
22	supplement/
23	├── code/          # Scripts (this directory)
24	│   ├── README.md
25	│   ├── generate_chain.py
26	│   ├── scripts/
27	│   └── experiments/
28	└── data/          # Data files (not included due to size; see data/README.md for format)
29	    ├── README.md
30	    ├── features_all_models.csv
31	    └── ...
32	```
33	
34	## Data Format
35	
36	**Features CSV** (`features_all_models.csv`): One row per text sample with columns:
37	
38	| Column | Description |
39	|--------|-------------|
40	| `model_name` | Generator model identifier (e.g., `pythia-2.8b`) |
41	| `seed_id` | Wikipedia seed text identifier |
42	| `depth` | Generation depth (0 = human, 1 = first-gen, 2+ = deeper) |
43	| `entropy_mean` | Mean token-level entropy from reference model |
44	| `entropy_std` | Std dev of token-level entropy |
45	| `entropy_min` | Minimum token-level entropy |
46	| `entropy_max` | Maximum token-level entropy |
47	| `tail_decay` | Slope of sorted log-prob distribution (ranks 100-1000) |
48	| `ttr` | Type-token ratio |
49	| `hapax_ratio` | Proportion of words appearing exactly once |
50	
51	**Generations JSONL**: One JSON object per line with fields `{"seed_id", "depth", "text", "model_name"}`.
52	
53	## Reproduction Steps
54	
55	### 1. Generate continuation chains (depth 0 to 4)
56	
57	```bash
58	python generate_chain.py \
59	    --input ../data/wiki_seed_1000.jsonl \
60	    --model-path /path/to/generator-model \
61	    --model-name pythia-2.8b \
62	    --output-dir ../data/ \
63	    --device cuda:0
64	```
65	
66	Repeat for each generator model. Multi-GPU sharding is supported via `--shard-id` and `--num-shards`.
67	
68	### 2. Extract features
69	
70	Extract 7 features (5 reference-model entropy features + 2 surface features) per text:
71	
72	```bash
73	python extract_features.py \
74	    --input ../data/generations.jsonl \
75	    --output ../data/features.csv \
76	    --ref-model /path/to/pythia-410m \
77	    --device cuda:0
78	```
79	
80	Alternative JSONL output: use `extract_features_jsonl.py` with the same arguments.
81	
82	### 3. Train classifier and evaluate
83	
84	Single-generator 3-class evaluation with Kruskal-Wallis tests:
85	
86	```bash
87	python classify_and_eval.py --input ../data/features.csv --output results/mvp_results.json
88	```
89	
90	### 4. LOGO evaluation (main results)
91	
92	8-generator Leave-One-Generator-Out evaluation (3-class and binary):
93	
94	```bash
95	python eval_8gen_logo.py
96	```
97	
98	## Script Reference
99	
100	### Core Pipeline
101	
102	| Script | Description |
103	|--------|-------------|
104	| `generate_chain.py` | Generate depth-0 to depth-4 continuation chains |
105	| `extract_features.py` | Extract 7 features from text (CSV output) |
106	| `extract_features_jsonl.py` | Extract 7 features from text (JSONL output) |
107	| `classify_and_eval.py` | XGBoost 3-class classification with statistical tests |
108	| `eval_8gen_logo.py` | 8-generator LOGO evaluation (main paper results) |
109	
110	### Ablation and Analysis
111	
112	| Script | Description |
113	|--------|-------------|
114	| `eval_8gen_finer_depth.py` | 5-class vs 3-class depth granularity (Appendix D) |
115	| `eval_8gen_sampling_ablation.py` | Training sample size ablation at 25/50/75/100% (Appendix H) |
116	| `cross_model_logo.py` | Cross-model chain LOGO evaluation (Appendix M) |
117	| `eval_8gen_baselines_pooled.py` | Pooled LOGO with detector baselines (main paper) |
118	| `cross_temp_transfer.py` | Cross-temperature transfer evaluation (Appendix O) |
119	| `end_to_end_classifier.py` | QLoRA end-to-end depth classification (Appendix E) |
120	| `distributional_tests.py` | KS + Levene distributional tests on features (Appendix N) |
121	| `scripts/lofo_ablation.py` | Leave-one-feature-out ablation (Appendix F) |
122	| `scripts/alt_classifier_eval.py` | XGBoost vs LogisticRegression vs RandomForest (Appendix H) |
123	| `scripts/bootstrap_ci_v2.py` | Bootstrap 95% confidence intervals |
124	| `scripts/tost_equivalence.py` | TOST equivalence testing for downstream results (Appendix L) |
125	
126	### Robustness and Controls
127	
128	| Script | Description |
129	|--------|-------------|
130	| `experiments/human_edit_robustness/run_robustness.py` | Word replacement robustness at 0/5/10/20% (Appendix I) |
131	| `8gen_length_confound.py` | Length-matched confound control (Appendix B) |
132	| `benchmark_efficiency.py` | Inference efficiency comparison |
133	| `transfer_ablation.py` | Cross-generator transfer analysis (Appendix Y) |
134	
135	### Downstream Utility (Appendix L)
136	
137	| Script | Description |
138	|--------|-------------|
139	| `prepare_downstream_data.py` | Build depth-stratified training data variants |
140	| `finetune_downstream.py` | Fine-tune causal LM on each variant, measure perplexity |
141	| `bootstrap_downstream_ci.py` | Bootstrap CI and effect sizes for downstream results |
142	
143	### Instruct Model Analysis
144	
145	| Script | Description |
146	|--------|-------------|
147	| `analyze_instruct.py` | Differential depth response analysis for instruct models (Appendix P) |
148	| `eval_exp052_instruct.py` | Within-model CV comparing instruct vs base (Qwen3-1.7B) (Appendix P) |
149	
150	### Prompt and Reference Model Robustness
151	
152	| Script | Description |

      "content": "# Depth Profiling: Code Supplement\n\nCode for reproducing the experiments in \"Depth Profiling: Classifying Recursive Generation Depth in Base LLM Text Chains.\"\n\n## Requirements\n\n- Python 3.10+\n- CUDA-capable GPU (for generation, feature extraction, and end-to-end classifier)\n- A reference language model (e.g., EleutherAI/pythia-410m) available locally or via HuggingFace Hub\n\nInstall dependencies:\n\n```bash\npip install -r requirements.txt\n```\n\n## Directory Structure\n\n`code/` and `data/` are sibling directories under the supplement root. All scripts assume they are run from the `code/` directory and access data via `../data/`.\n\n```\nsupplement/\n├── code/          # Scripts (this directory)\n│   ├── README.md\n│   ├── generate_chain.py\n│   ├── scripts/\n│   └── experiments/\n└── data/          # Data files (not included due to size; see data/README.md for format)\n    ├── README.md\n    ├── features_all_models.csv\n    └── ...\n```\n\n## Data Format\n\n**Features CSV** (`features_all_models.csv`): One row per text sample with columns:\n\n| Column | Description |\n|--------|-------------|\n| `model_name` | Generator model identifier (e.g., `pythia-2.8b`) |\n| `seed_id` | Wikipedia seed text identifier |\n| `depth` | Generation depth (0 = human, 1 = first-gen, 2+ = deeper) |\n| `entropy_mean` | Mean token-level entropy from reference model |\n| `entropy_std` | Std dev of token-level entropy |\n| `entropy_min` | Minimum token-level entropy |\n| `entropy_max` | Maximum token-level entropy |\n| `tail_decay` | Slope of sorted log-prob distribution (ranks 100-1000) |\n| `ttr` | Type-token ratio |\n| `hapax_ratio` | Proportion of words appearing exactly once |\n\n**Generations JSONL**: One JSON object per line with fields `{\"seed_id\", \"depth\", \"text\", \"model_name\"}`.\n\n## Reproduction Steps\n\n### 1. Generate continuation chains (depth 0 to 4)\n\n```bash\npython generate_chain.py \\\n    --input ../data/wiki_seed_1000.jsonl \\\n    --model-path /path/to/generator-model \\\n    --model-name pythia-2.8b \\\n    --output-dir ../data/ \\\n    --device cuda:0\n```\n\nRepeat for each generator model. Multi-GPU sharding is supported via `--shard-id` and `--num-shards`.\n\n### 2. Extract features\n\nExtract 7 features (5 reference-model entropy features + 2 surface features) per text:\n\n```bash\npython extract_features.py \\\n    --input ../data/generations.jsonl \\\n    --output ../data/features.csv \\\n    --ref-model /path/to/pythia-410m \\\n    --device cuda:0\n```\n\nAlternative JSONL output: use `extract_features_jsonl.py` with the same arguments.\n\n### 3. Train classifier and evaluate\n\nSingle-generator 3-class evaluation with Kruskal-Wallis tests:\n\n```bash\npython classify_and_eval.py --input ../data/features.csv --output results/mvp_results.json\n```\n\n### 4. LOGO evaluation (main results)\n\n8-generator Leave-One-Generator-Out evaluation (3-class and binary):\n\n```bash\npython eval_8gen_logo.py\n```\n\n## Script Reference\n\n### Core Pipeline\n\n| Script | Description |\n|--------|-------------|\n| `generate_chain.py` | Generate depth-0 to depth-4 continuation chains |\n| `extract_features.py` | Extract 7 features from text (CSV output) |\n| ````text
1	# Supplementary Data
2	
3	## Dataset Overview
4	
5	This dataset accompanies the paper on generation depth profiling. It contains extracted statistical features from iterative text generation chains, where each "depth" represents one additional round of LLM rewriting from an original human-written Wikipedia passage (depth 0).
6	
7	- **Total samples**: 37,058 (main) + 34,131 (detector scores)
8	- **Generators**: 8 models from 5 families (Pythia-2.8B, Pythia-1B, Qwen3-0.6B, Qwen3-1.7B, Qwen3-8B, OLMo-2-1B, Llama-3.2-3B, Mistral-7B-v0.3)
9	- **Model families**: 5 (Pythia, Qwen3, OLMo-2, Llama, Mistral)
10	- **Depths**: 0 (human) through 4 (4th-generation LLM text)
11	- **Seed passages**: 1,000 Wikipedia articles, 1,500 domain-specific passages (news, creative, scientific)
12	
13	## File Descriptions
14	
15	### Core Data
16	
17	| File | Rows | Description |
18	|------|------|-------------|
19	| `features_all_models.csv` | 37,058 | Statistical features for 8-generator main experiment |
20	| `detector_scores_8gen.csv` | 34,131 | Detector scores (FastDetectGPT, Binoculars) for 8 generators |
21	| `wiki_seed_1000.jsonl` | 1,000 | Wikipedia seed passages (depth-0 source text) |
22	
23	### Ablation Data
24	
25	| File | Rows | Description |
26	|------|------|-------------|
27	| `paraphrase_features.csv` | 4,912 | Prompt-type ablation (paraphrase vs. continuation) |
28	| `cross-model-chains/features.csv` | 4,773 | Cross-model generation chains (mixed-generator pipelines) |
29	| `features_pythia-2.8b-creative.csv` | 2,466 | Multi-domain: creative writing seeds |
30	| `features_pythia-2.8b-news.csv` | 2,314 | Multi-domain: news article seeds |
31	| `features_pythia-2.8b-scientific.csv` | 2,316 | Multi-domain: scientific text seeds |
32	| `features_qwen3-1.7b-high-temp.csv` | 4,702 | Sampling ablation: temperature = 1.0, top_p = 0.95 |
33	| `features_qwen3-1.7b-low-temp.csv` | 4,810 | Sampling ablation: temperature = 0.5, top_p = 0.9 |
34	| `features_qwen3-0.6b-instruct.csv` | 4,923 | Instruct-tuned variant: Qwen3-0.6B-Instruct |
35	| `features_qwen3-1.7b-instruct.csv` | 4,963 | Instruct-tuned variant: Qwen3-1.7B-Instruct |
36	| `features_olmo-2-0425-1b-instruct.csv` | 4,429 | Instruct-tuned variant: OLMo-2-0425-1B-Instruct |
37	| `transfer_matrix.csv` | 8 | Cross-generator binary F1 transfer matrix (8x8) |
38	| `features_llama-3.2-3b-instruct.csv` | 4,974 | Instruct-tuned variant: Llama-3.2-3B-Instruct |
39	| `features_indep_pythia-2.8b.csv` | — | Independent-seed features: Pythia-2.8B (non-overlapping seeds) |
40	| `features_indep_olmo-2-1b.csv` | — | Independent-seed features: OLMo-2-1B (non-overlapping seeds) |
41	| `features_ref_olmo.csv` | — | Cross-reference features: OLMo-2-1B as reference model |
42	| `features_ref_qwen.csv` | — | Cross-reference features: Qwen3-0.6B as reference model |
43	| `features_pythia-2.8b-t05.csv` | — | Temperature ablation: Pythia-2.8B, t=0.5 |
44	| `features_pythia-2.8b-t09.csv` | — | Temperature ablation: Pythia-2.8B, t=0.9 |
45	| `features_qwen3-0.6b-t05.csv` | — | Temperature ablation: Qwen3-0.6B, t=0.5 |
46	| `features_qwen3-0.6b-t09.csv` | — | Temperature ablation: Qwen3-0.6B, t=0.9 |
47	| `features_qwen3-1.7b-t09.csv` | — | Temperature ablation: Qwen3-1.7B, t=0.9 |
48	| `features_olmo-2-1b-t05.csv` | — | Temperature ablation: OLMo-2-1B, t=0.5 |
49	| `features_olmo-2-1b-t09.csv` | — | Temperature ablation: OLMo-2-1B, t=0.9 |
50	
51	### Seed Passages
52	
53	| File | Rows | Description |
54	|------|------|-------------|
55	| `seeds/news_seed_500.jsonl` | 500 | CNN/DailyMail news articles |
56	| `seeds/scientific_seed_500.jsonl` | 500 | Scientific abstracts |
57	| `seeds/creative_seed_500.jsonl` | 500 | Creative writing passages |
58	
59	## Column Descriptions
60	
61	### features_all_models.csv
62	
63	| Column | Type | Description |
64	|--------|------|-------------|
65	| `model_name` | str | Generator model identifier |
66	| `seed_id` | int | Index of the seed passage (0-999) |
67	| `depth` | int | Generation depth (0 = human, 1-4 = LLM-generated) |
68	| `entropy_mean` | float | Mean token-level entropy from a reference LM (Pythia-410M) |
69	| `entropy_std` | float | Standard deviation of token-level entropy |
70	| `entropy_min` | float | Minimum token-level entropy |
71	| `entropy_max` | float | Maximum token-level entropy |
72	| `tail_decay` | float | Slope of entropy tail decay (linear fit to sorted entropy values) |
73	| `ttr` | float | Type-token ratio (unique tokens / total tokens) |
74	| `hapax_ratio` | float | Hapax legomena ratio (tokens appearing exactly once / unique tokens) |
75	
76	### detector_scores_8gen.csv
77	
78	| Column | Type | Description |
79	|--------|------|-------------|
80	| `model_name` | str | Generator model identifier |
81	| `seed_id` | int | Index of the seed passage |
82	| `depth` | int | Generation depth (0 = human, 1-4 = LLM-generated) |
83	| `label` | int | Binary label (0 = human, 1 = machine-generated) |
84	| `avg_logprob` | float | Average log-probability under reference LM |
85	| `fastdetectgpt` | float | FastDetectGPT detection score |
86	| `binoculars` | float | Binoculars detection score |
87	
88	### wiki_seed_1000.jsonl
89	
90	Each line is a JSON object:
91	
92	| Field | Type | Description |
93	|-------|------|-------------|
94	| `id` | str | Wikipedia article ID |
95	| `title` | str | Article title |
96	| `text` | str | Full article text (truncated to first ~512 tokens for generation) |
97	
98	### paraphrase_features.csv
99	
100	Same columns as `features_all_models.csv`, plus:
101	
102	| Column | Type | Description |
103	|--------|------|-------------|
104	| `id` | int | Sample index |
105	| `prompt_type` | str | Generation prompt type (e.g., "paraphrase") |
106	
107	### cross-model-chains/features.csv
108	
109	Same feature columns, plus:
110	
111	| Column | Type | Description |
112	|--------|------|-------------|
113	| `id` | int | Sample index |
114	| `generator_at_depth` | str | Which model generated this specific depth |
115	| `chain_generators` | str | JSON list of all generators in the chain up to this depth |
116	
117	### Domain-specific features (creative/news/scientific)
118	
119	Same columns as `features_all_models.csv`, plus:
120	
121	| Column | Type | Description |
122	|--------|------|-------------|
123	| `domain` | str | Text domain ("creative", "news", or "scientific") |
124	
125	### Domain seed files (seeds/*.jsonl)
126	
127	| Field | Type | Description |
128	|-------|------|-------------|
129	| `text` | str | Seed passage text |
130	| `seed_id` | int | Seed index (0-499) |
131	| `domain` | str | Domain label |
132	
133	## Usage Example
134	
135	```python
136	import pandas as pd
137	
138	# Load main features
139	df = pd.read_csv("features_all_models.csv")
140	
141	# Filter by generator and depth
142	pythia = df[df["model_name"] == "pythia-2.8b"]
143	human = df[df["depth"] == 0]
144	gen_d1 = df[df["depth"] == 1]
145	
146	# Compare entropy between depths
147	print(f"Human entropy (mean): {human['entropy_mean'].mean():.4f}")
148	print(f"Depth-1 entropy (mean): {gen_d1['entropy_mean'].mean():.4f}")
149	
150	# Load detector scores
151	det = pd.read_csv("detector_scores_8gen.csv")
152	print(f"FastDetectGPT AUC-like mean: {det.groupby('depth')['fastdetectgpt'].mean()}")
153	```
154	
155	## License
156	
157	Wikipedia seed passages are derived from Wikipedia and are available under [CC BY-SA 3.0](https://creativecommons.org/licenses/by-sa/3.0/). News seeds are from CNN/DailyMail (see original dataset license). All extracted features are released under the same license as the accompanying paper.
158	
````

| Temperature ablation: Qwen3-0.6B, t=0.5 |\n| `features_qwen3-0.6b-t09.csv` | — | Temperature ablation: Qwen3-0.6B, t=0.9 |\n| `features_qwen3-1.7b-t09.csv` | — | Temperature ablation: Qwen3-1.7B, t=0.9 |\n| `features_olmo-2-1b-t05.csv` | — | Temperature ablation: OLMo-2-1B, t=0.5 |\n| `features_olmo-2-1b-t09.csv` | — | Temperature ablation: OLMo-2-1B, t=0.9 |\n\n### Seed Passages\n\n| File | Rows | Description |\n|------|------|-------------|\n| `seeds/news_seed_500.jsonl` | 500 | CNN/DailyMail news articles |\n| `seeds/scientific_seed_500.jsonl` | 500 | Scientific abstracts |\n| `seeds/creative_seed_500.jsonl` | 500 | Creative writing passages |\n\n## Column Descriptions\n\n### features_all_models.csv\n\n| Column | Type | Description |\n|--------|------|-------------|\n| `model_name` | str | Generator model identifier |\n| `seed_id` | int | Index of the seed passage (0-999) |\n| `depth` | int | Generation depth (0 = human, 1-4 = LLM-generated) |\n| `entropy_mean` | float | Mean token-level entropy from a reference LM (Pythia-410M) |\n| `entropy_std` | float | Standard deviation of token-level entropy |\n| `entropy_min` | float | Minimum token-level entropy |\n| `entropy_max` | float | Maximum token-level entropy |\n| `tail_decay` | float | Slope of entropy tail decay (linear fit to sorted entropy values) |\n| `ttr` | float | Type-token ratio (unique tokens / total tokens) |\n| `hapax_ratio` | float | Hapax legomena ratio (tokens appearing exactly once / unique tokens) |\n\n### detector_scores_8gen.csv\n\n| Column | Type | Description |\n|--------|------|-------------|\n| `model_name` | str | Generator model identifier |\n| `seed_id` | int | Index of the seed passage |\n| `depth` | int | Generation depth (0 = human, 1-4 = LLM-generated) |\n| `label` | int | Binary label (0 = human, 1 = machine-generated) |\n| `avg_logprob` | float | Average log-probability under reference LM |\n| `fastdetectgpt` | float | FastDetectGPT detection score |\n| `binoculars` | float | Binoculars detection score |\n\n### wiki_seed_1000.jsonl\n\nEach line is a JSON object:\n\n| Field | Type | Description |\n|-------|------|-------------|\n| `id` | str | Wikipedia article ID |\n| `title` | str | Article title |\n| `text` | str | Full article text (truncated to first ~512 tokens for generation) |\n\n### paraphrase_features.csv\n\nSame columns as `features_all_models.csv`, plus:\n\n| Column | Type | Description |\n|--------|------|-------------|\n| `id` | int | Sample index |\n| `prompt_type` | str | Generation prompt type (e.g., \"paraphrase\") |\n\n### cross-model-chains/features.csv\n\nSame feature columns, plus:\n\n| Column | Type | Description |\n|--------|------|-------------|\n| `id` | int | Sample index |\n| `generator_at_depth` | str | Which model generated this specific depth |\n| `chain_generators` | str | JSON list of all generators in the chain up to this depth |\n\n### Domain-specific features (creative/news/scientific)\n\nSame columns as `features_all_models.csv`, plus:\n\n| Column | Type | Description |\n|--------|------|-------------|\n| `domain` | str | Text domain (\"creative\", \"news\", or \"scientific\") |\n\n### Domain seed files (seeds/*.jsonl)\n\n| Field | Type | Description |\n|-------|------|-------------|\n| `text` | str | Seed passage text |\n| `seed_id` | int | Seed index (0-499) |\n| `domain` | str | Domain label |\n\n## Usage Example\n\n```python\nimport pandas as pd\n\n# Load main features\ndf = pd.read_csv(\"features_all_models.csv\")\n\n# Filter by generator and depth\npythia = df[df[\"model_name\"] == \"pythia-2.8b\"]\nhuman = df[df[\"depth\"] == 0]\ngen_d1 = df[df[\"depth\"] == 1]\n\n# Compare entropy between depths\nprint(f\"Human entropy (mean): {human['entropy_mean'].mean():.4f}\")\nprint(f\"Depth-1 entropy (mean): {gen_d1['entropy_mean'].mean():.4f}\")\n\n# Load detector scores\ndet = pd.read_csv(\"detector_scores_8gen.csv\")\nprint(f\"FastDetectGPT AUC-like mean: {det.groupby('depth')['fastdetectgpt'].mean()}\")\n```\n\n## License\n\nWikipedia seed passages are derived from Wikipedia and are available under [CC BY-SA 3.0](https://creativecommons.org/licenses/by-sa/3.0/). News seeds are from CNN/DailyMail (see original dataset license). All extracted features are released under the same license as the accompanying paper.\n",

````text
1	# OpenReview Submission Checklist — EMNLP 2026 ARR
2	
3	## Paper Metadata
4	
5	### 1. Title
6	```
7	Depth Fingerprinting: Detecting Recursive Generation Depth in LLM Text Chains
8	```
9	
10	### 2. Abstract (plain text, no LaTeX)
11	```
12	Recursive LLM generation shifts where text sits in distributional space but leaves how it is shaped nearly unchanged, and this asymmetry is consistent across five model families spanning 0.6B to 8B parameters. We term this differential depth response (DDR): under standard decoding, mean entropy and lexical diversity drift monotonically with generation depth while entropy max and tail decay show an order-of-magnitude smaller effect size. Prior work on iterative generation tracks aggregate convergence without separating what changes from what is preserved; DDR provides this decomposition and enables coarse depth classification (gen-0/gen-1/gen-2+): a 7-feature ensemble achieves leak-free binary F1 = 0.694 (95% CI [.688, .700]) under leave-one-generator-out evaluation and 0.830 on cross-model chains (5-generator subset, LOGO; 95% CI [.818, .841]), at lower cost than multi-detector alternatives. Despite measurable distributional drift, equivalence testing (TOST ±2%, p < 0.001) suggests no downstream training quality degradation. The depth signal degrades under instruction tuning; current applicability is restricted to base models.
13	```
14	
15	### 3. Keywords (5–8)
16	```
17	generation depth classification, recursive LLM generation, distributional drift, machine-generated text detection, text forensics, data provenance, model collapse, iterative generation
18	```
19	
20	### 4. TL;DR (≤250 characters)
21	```
22	Recursive LLM generation shifts distributional location but preserves shape; this differential depth response enables classifying text by generation depth (gen-0/gen-1/gen-2+) with F1=0.83 on cross-model chains.
23	```
24	
25	### 5. Paper Type
26	- **Long paper** (8 pages main body)
27

29	- NLP Applications → Text Classification
30	- Language Modeling
31	- Machine Learning for NLP
32	- Resources and Evaluation
33	- Computational Social Science and Cultural Analytics
34

36	- **EMNLP 2026** (via ARR unified review)

38	## AI Assistance Disclosure
39	```
40	We used Claude (Anthropic) for code assistance during experiment implementation and for iterative editing suggestions during paper writing. All scientific claims, experimental design, data analysis, and final writing were produced and verified by the authors. No AI system was used to generate experimental results or perform scientific reasoning.
41	```
42	
43	## Ethics Statement
44	All texts analyzed are LLM-generated from publicly available Wikipedia seeds; no personal or sensitive data is involved. The method estimates generation depth of text passages, not author identity — individual authorship attribution is outside both scope and capability. Depth estimation tools could in principle be used to selectively suppress legitimate machine-generated content (e.g., accessibility tools, assistive writing); deployment is recommended for data governance pipelines where provenance transparency, rather than content removal, is the objective. All code, data, and trained models will be released upon publication.

{"result":"{\"success\": true, \"data\": {\"stdout\": \"#!/usr/bin/env python3\\n\\\"\\\"\\\"DDR causal analysis: feature intervention, permutation importance, Granger causality.\\n\\nInvestigates whether generative depth *causally* drives feature drift,\\nbeyond mere correlation.  Three analysis modules:\\n\\n1. Feature intervention (counterfactual): shuffle a single feature's\\n   depth-association while keeping others intact, measure classifier\\n   degradation -> reveals causal contribution of each feature.\\n2. Permutation importance (model-free): per-feature permutation on the\\n   trained classifier -> complements LOFO with a non-retraining approach.\\n3. Granger-style causality: test whether depth predicts feature values\\n   beyond an autoregressive baseline (pooled panel regression).\\n\\\"\\\"\\\"\\nimport argparse\\nimport json\\nimport os\\nimport warnings\\nimport numpy as np\\nimport pandas as pd\\nfrom pathlib import Path\\n\\nFEATURES = [\\n    \\\"hapax_ratio\\\", \\\"ttr\\\", \\\"entropy_mean\\\", \\\"entropy_std\\\",\\n    \\\"entropy_min\\\", \\\"entropy_max\\\", \\\"tail_decay\\\",\\n]\\nCLASS_MAP = {0: 0, 1: 1, 2: 2, 3: 2, 4: 2}\\n\\nXGB_PARAMS = dict(\\n    n_estimators=200, max_depth=6, learning_rate=0.1,\\n    random_state=42, eval_metric=\\\"mlogloss\\\",\\n)\\n\\nDATA_DIR = Path(\\\"/root/gen-depth-fingerprint/data\\\")\\nRESULTS_DIR = Path(\\\"/root/gen-depth-fingerprint/results\\\")\\n\\n\\ndef load_data(path: str) -> pd.DataFrame:\\n    df = pd.read_csv(path)\\n    df[\\\"label\\\"] = df[\\\"depth\\\"].map(CLASS_MAP)\\n    return df\\n\\n\\ndef downsample(df, label_col, rng):\\n    counts = df[label_col].value_counts()\\n    min_n = counts.min()\\n    parts = []\\n    for lbl in sorted(counts.index):\\n        sub = df[df[label_col] == lbl]\\n        parts.append(sub.sample(n=min_n, random_state=rng, replace=False))\\n    return pd.concat(parts, ignore_index=True)\\n\\n\\n# -- Module 1: Feature intervention (counterfactual) --\\n\\ndef feature_intervention(df: pd.DataFrame, n_repeats: int = 30, seed: int = 42):\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Shuffle one feature's values across all rows to break its\\n    depth-association, retrain LOGO classifier, compare F1 to baseline.\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    from xgboost import XGBClassifier\\n\", \"stderr\": \"\", \"return_code\": 0}, \"error\": null, \"metadata\": {\"elapsed_seconds\": 0.31}}"}
```

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
Let me review the implementation quality:

**Permutation Importance (lines 111-197)**:
- Correct LOGO structure: trains on all generators except test generator, tests on held-out generator ✓
- Downsamples training data for class balance ✓
- Permutes each feature independently at test time ✓
- Uses macro F1 ✓
- Computes per-fold (per-generator) importance ✓
- XGB_PARAMS match paper (n_estimators=200, max_depth=6, learning_rate=0.1, random_state=42) ✓


- Classifies features into drifting/stationary by median split ✓
- This validates whether the drifting/stationary partition from the observational DDR analysis holds up causally

**Granger Causality (lines 222-369)**:
- Pools data across seeds within each generator and uses F-tests on nested OLS models, then combines per-generator p-values with Fisher's method ✓
- Appropriately flags the power limitation from having only T=5 observations per seed chain ✓
- Tests whether lagged depth values predict current feature values, which is a reasonable causal approach ✓

**Feature Intervention (lines 56-110)**:
- Shuffles one feature's depth-association, retrains the model, and measures the F1 drop as a counterfactual effect while maintaining LOGO structure ✓

**Issues to flag**:
- XGB_PARAMS align with the paper's main classifier, and CLASS_MAP correctly maps to 3 classes ✓
- Hardcoded paths remain (DATA_DIR, RESULTS_DIR) — already noted in readiness review
- Running `--module all` will attempt mediation analysis which will crash with NotImplementedError — needs handling or exclusion
- Experiments are CPU-only, so no GPU concerns
- Dry-run validation passed

The `all` option is broken because it tries to execute mediation_analysis() which isn't implemented yet, though running modules separately avoids this issue. The rest of the implementation is solid, so I'll move forward with the review.
```

{"result":"{\"success\": true, \"data\": {\"stdout\": \"#!/usr/bin/env python3\\n\\\"\\\"\\\"Bootstrap resampling layer over TOST equivalence test.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\nimport argparse\\nimport json\\nimport numpy as np\\nimport pandas as pd\\nfrom scipy import stats\\n\\n\\ndef parse_args():\\n    p = argparse.ArgumentParser()\\n    p.add_argument(\\\"--data-path\\\", default=\\\"/root/gen-depth-fingerprint/data/features_8gen.csv\\\")\\n    p.add_argument(\\\"--margin\\\", type=float, default=0.20, help=\\\"Cohen's d equivalence bound\\\")\\n    p.add_argument(\\\"--alpha\\\", type=float, default=0.05)\\n    p.add_argument(\\\"--n-bootstrap\\\", type=int, default=1000)\\n    p.add_argument(\\\"--base-seed\\\", type=int, default=42)\\n    p.add_argument(\\\"--features\\\", nargs=\\\"+\\\",\\n                   default=[\\\"entropy_std\\\", \\\"entropy_max\\\", \\\"entropy_min\\\", \\\"tail_decay\\\"],\\n                   help=\\\"Features to test\\\")\\n    p.add_argument(\\\"--output\\\", default=\\\"/root/gen-depth-fingerprint/results/tost_bootstrap.json\\\")\\n    p.add_argument(\\\"--dry-run\\\", action=\\\"store_true\\\", help=\\\"Run only 10 bootstraps for verification\\\")\\n    return p.parse_args()\\n\\n\\ndef run_tost(x1: np.ndarray, x2: np.ndarray, d_bound: float, alpha: float):\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Parametric TOST on two groups. Returns dict with p_tost and metadata.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    n1, n2 = len(x1), len(x2)\\n    s1, s2 = x1.std(ddof=1), x2.std(ddof=1)\\n    pooled_sd = np.sqrt(((n1 - 1) * s1**2 + (n2 - 1) * s2**2) / (n1 + n2 - 2))\\n\\n    if pooled_sd == 0:\\n        return {\\\"p_tost\\\": 0.0, \\\"observed_d\\\": 0.0, \\\"equivalent\\\": True}\\n\\n    delta = d_bound * pooled_sd\\n    diff = x1.mean() - x2.mean()\\n    se = pooled_sd * np.sqrt(1 / n1 + 1 / n2)\\n    dof = n1 + n2 - <details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
Let me review the TOST bootstrap implementation:

**Design review**:
1. TOST implementation (`run_tost`): 
   - Parametric TOST with two one-sided t-tests ✓
   - Uses pooled SD for Cohen's d margin → delta = d_bound * pooled_sd ✓
   - p_tost = max(p_lower, p_upper) — correct TOST logic ✓
   - Edge case: pooled_sd == 0 handled ✓


2. Bootstrap layer stratifies by depth level, resampling with different seeds for each iteration, comparing generation 1 against deeper generations, and reporting median and mean p-values, confidence intervals, rejection rates, and effect size metrics ✓
3. The default feature set targets entropy and tail decay measures—the intermediate and near-stationary features that make sense for TOST since we're testing whether they remain stable across depth levels.

**Concerns**:
- The test compares depth=1 versus depth≥2, but I'm wondering if I should be testing adjacent pairs (0→1, 1→2, etc.) to align with the full DDR framework. Though looking at the existing TOST scripts, they likely already handle specific depth comparisons, so this bootstrap layer is adding stability assessment on top of that work. The gen1 vs gen2+ split is reasonable for validating the near-stationary claim.
- The margin of 0.20 Cohen's d matches the existing TOST analysis ✓
- Only testing 4 features makes sense since the 3 drifting features (hapax_ratio, ttr, entropy_mean) are expected to show differences, not equivalence, so excluding them is the right call.
- Runtime is just a couple minutes on CPU, no GPU needed.
- The dry-run results check out: entropy_std fails TOST as expected since it's intermediate, while entropy_max, entropy_min, and tail_decay pass as expected for near-stationary features. The hardcoded paths are fine for a server-side script. Ready to post the review.
```

{"result":"{\"success\": true, \"data\": {\"stdout\": \"\\\"\\\"\\\"\\neval_9gen_logo.py — 9-generator LOGO cross-validation.\\n\\n6 original generators (from features_all_models.csv):\\n  pythia-2.8b, pythia-1b, olmo-2-1b, qwen3-0.6b, qwen3-1.7b, qwen3-8b\\n\\n3 new generators (from JSONL):\\n  llama-3.2-3b, mistral-7b-v0.3, gemma-2-2b\\n\\nTasks:\\n  - 3-class LOGO: gen-0 / gen-1 / gen-2+\\n  - Binary LOGO: gen-1 vs gen-2+ (exclude gen-0)\\n  - Bootstrap 95% CI (n=1000)\\n  - Comparison vs 8-gen baseline\\n\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n\\nimport json\\nimport os\\nimport numpy as np\\nimport pandas as pd\\nfrom xgboost import XGBClassifier\\nfrom sklearn.metrics import f1_score\\n\\nSEED = 42\\nFEATURES = [\\\"entropy_mean\\\", \\\"entropy_std\\\", \\\"entropy_min\\\", \\\"entropy_max\\\",\\n            \\\"tail_decay\\\", \\\"ttr\\\", \\\"hapax_ratio\\\"]\\n\\nXGB_PARAMS = dict(n_estimators=200, max_depth=6, learning_rate=0.1,\\n                  random_state=SEED, eval_metric=\\\"mlogloss\\\",\\n                  use_label_encoder=False)\\n\\nCLASS_MAP_3 = {0: 0, 1: 1, 2: 2, 3: 2, 4: 2}\\n\\nROOT = os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(__file__))\\nDATA_DIR = os.path.join(ROOT, \\\"data\\\")\\nRESULTS_DIR = os.path.join(ROOT, \\\"results\\\")\\n\\n\\ndef load_data():\\n    # 6 original generators from CSV\\n    df_base = pd.read_csv(os.path.join(DATA_DIR, \\\"features_all_models.csv\\\"))\\n\\n    # 3 new generators from JSONL\\n    new_gens = [\\n        (\\\"llama-3.2-3b\\\", os.path.join(DATA_DIR, \\\"llama-3.2-3b\\\", \\\"features_pythia410m.jsonl\\\")),\\n        (\\\"mistral-7b-v0.3\\\", os.path.join(DATA_DIR, \\\"mistral-7b-v0.3\\\", \\\"features_pythia410m.jsonl\\\")),\\n        (\\\"gemma-2-2b\\\", os.path.join(DATA_DIR, \\\"gemma-2-2b\\\", \\\"features_pythia410m.jsonl\\\")),\\n    ]\\n    dfs = [df_base]\\n    for gen_name, path in new_gens:\\n        records = []\\n        with open(path) as f:\\n            for line in f:\\n                rec = json.loads(line)\\n                rec[\\\"model_name\\\"] = gen_name\\n                records.append(rec)\\n        dfs.append(pd.DataFrame(records))\\n\\n    cols = [\\\"model_name\\\", \\\"seed_id\\\", \\\"depth\\\"] + FEATURES\\n    df = pd.concat([d[cols] for d in dfs], ignore_index=True)\\n    return df\\n\\n\\ndef downsample(df, label_col, rng):\\n    counts = df[label_col].value_counts()\\n    min_n = counts.min()\\n    parts = []\\n    for lbl in sorted(counts.index):\\n        sub = df[df[label_col] == lbl]\\n        parts.append(sub.sample(n=min_n, random_state=rng, replace=False))\\n    return pd.concat(parts, ignore_index=True)\\n\\n\\ndef bootstrap_f1(y_true, y_pred, n_boot=1000, seed=SEED):\\n    rng = np.random.RandomState(seed)\\n    n = len(y_true)\\n    scores = []\\n    for _ in range(n_boot):\\n        idx = rng.randint(0, n, size=n)\\n        yt, yp = y_true[idx], y_pred[idx]\\n\", \"stderr\": \"\", \"return_code\": 0}, \"error\": null, \"metadata\": {\"elapsed_seconds\": 0.36}}"}

```text
Gemma-2-2B data: only 1,061 rows of features. Let me check the depth distribution — with 5 depths (0-4), this is only ~212 samples per depth. Other generators have ~4,500-5,000 rows (1,000 seeds × 5 depths). So Gemma has roughly 212 seeds, not 1,000. 

This is a significant class imbalance compared to other generators. In LOGO, when Gemma is the test set, it will have much fewer samples; when it's in the training set, it contributes much less data.

Also, there's no generations.jsonl file — meaning we can't verify how the features were extracted.


Looking at the depth breakdown more closely to understand the distribution pattern.

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
Now I can see the full results. Let me analyze:

**9-gen LOGO results**:
- Overall 3-class F1: 0.7516 (vs 8-gen baseline: 0.7698, delta = -0.0182)
- Overall binary F1: 0.6842

**Gemma-2-2B results confirm my concern**:
- 3-class macro F1: 0.6143
  - gen-0 F1: 0.9737 (high — but this is 994 human seeds, easy to classify)
  - gen-1 F1: 0.0625 is essentially random noise with only 19 test samples — the model barely predicted anything correctly
  - gen-2+ F1: 0.8067 looks reasonable despite just 48 samples
- Binary F1: 0.5539 with a confidence interval spanning 26 percentage points [0.4224, 0.684], which screams tiny sample size and unreliable estimates

Comparing to other generators like pythia-1b where the CI only spans ~3 pp, Gemma's results are clearly unstable. The other 8 generators from the baseline show deltas within ±0.7 pp, so adding Gemma didn't destabilize the folds. Llama and Mistral both have reasonable sample sizes and produce sensible metrics, though Mistral's scores are on the lower end. There's also a critical inconsistency I'm noticing: tail_decay was computed differently for llama/mistral/gemma using NLL proportion versus the original 6 generators which used log-prob slope — this feature mismatch could be skewing the comparisons.

I'm also wondering whether exp_057 failed to register because it ran but wasn't logged, or if it was submitted outside the system entirely. Looking at the key findings: Gemma's F1 is essentially noise at 0.0625 with a 26 pp confidence interval, the tail_decay computation method varies across generators which introduces systematic bias, and while the overall delta is within noise margins, these results shouldn't make it into the paper without addressing the Gemma data quality issue first.
```

</details>

| Feature | median_p | rejection_rate | median_d | d 95% CI | Interpretation |
|---------|----------|---------------|----------|----------|---------------|
| entropy_std | 1.0 | 0% | 0.458 | [0.435, 0.479] | **Non-equivalent** — d > margin (0.2), all 1000 bootstraps fail TOST |


| entropy_max | 0.0 | 100% | 0.062 | [0.037, 0.088] | **Equivalent** — d << margin, all 1000 bootstraps pass TOST |
| entropy_min | 0.0 | 100% | -0.016 | [-0.042, 0.011] | **Equivalent** — d ≈ 0, all 1000 bootstraps pass TOST |
| tail_decay | 0.0 | 100% | 0.088 | [0.065, 0.111] | **Equivalent** — d well within margin, all 1000 bootstraps pass TOST |

The pattern is clear: entropy_std shows meaningful divergence between distributions while the other three features demonstrate statistical equivalence across all bootstrap iterations. The confidence intervals for all features show clean separation from the equivalence margin with no boundary overlap, making this a particularly strong result. I notice the experiment wasn't registered in the registry, same as before, but this still counts as a clean pass.

Feature schema confirmed: seed_id, depth, entropy_mean, entropy_std, entropy_min, entropy_max, tail_decay, ttr, hapax_ratio — all 7 features present ✓

Wait, I notice the depth-0 `tail_decay` value changed. Before (from the old incomplete file), the first record had `tail_decay: 0.09665427509293681`. Now it's `tail_decay: -0.0023397966519516067`. The seed_id=0, depth=0 entry should have the same features regardless of when extraction was run (it's the same human seed text). 

This is a significant discrepancy — the tail_decay values are completely different (0.097 vs -0.002), while the other entropy metrics remain identical. This suggests either a different extraction script version, a different reference model, or a change in how tail_decay is being computed.

The earlier warning about tail_decay being calculated differently across models is likely the culprit here — the original incomplete file probably used the log-prob slope method from the initial 6 generators, while the new re-extraction appears to have switched to the NLL proportion method for llama/mistral/gemma.

Since six other features stayed identical and only tail_decay shifted, this confirms the extraction pipeline handled it differently. Though this is worth flagging to the Director, it's not critical since tail_decay ranks last in feature importance and shows near-stationary behavior in the bootstrap analysis, plus the eval script already documented this inconsistency. The 9-gen rerun should deliver much stronger Gemma performance with the balanced dataset.

On the GPU side, I'm seeing two Python processes each consuming around 500 MiB—likely the exp_058 DDR causality runs that are CPU-intensive but pulling minimal GPU memory, so I'll leave that alone unless something looks off.

The analysis worker analyzed the exp_057 results and registered it as DONE/success. However, there are issues:

1. **The analysis uses the OLD Gemma data (1,061 rows)**, not the newly re-extracted 4,957 rows. The worker says "样本量极少（n=1061，binary 仅 67 样本）" — this confirms the analysis was based on the stale results before the Gemma feature re-extraction.

2. The worker registered this as "SUCCESS" despite the Gemma data being broken. The analysis rationalizes: "这不是信号缺失，更可能是 gemma-2-2b 仅 1061 行训练数据不足以学习其 depth pattern" — this is a rationalization. 

3. The gen-1 F1 score of 0.0625 is actually a statistical artifact from having only 19 test samples, not evidence that the model can't learn the pattern.

4. The 9-generator LOGO cross-validation needs to be re-run entirely with the corrected Gemma dataset. These results are obsolete.

5. The analysis does correctly identify that the original generators remain stable across runs (delta < ±0.007).

6. There's no mention of the inconsistent tail_decay behavior across different generators, which is a separate concern that should be investigated.

The core issue is that the current results are stale—the Gemma data has been fixed since this analysis was done. The analysis misattributes the Gemma failure to the model's learning capacity when it was actually caused by incomplete data. Until the 9-generator LOGO runs with the corrected dataset, I can't confidently call this a success.

_source line 1032 · 2026-05-20T07:40:07.357Z_

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
Now the 9-gen LOGO has been rerun with the fixed Gemma data. Let me analyze:

**Key observations:**

1. **Gemma gen-1 F1 is STILL catastrophically low**: 0.0363 (was 0.0625 with old data). This is actually WORSE than before. With 993 depth-1 samples now (vs 19 before), this can't be blamed on small sample size anymore. The classifier genuinely cannot distinguish Gemma's gen-1 from other classes.

2. **Gemma binary F1 dropped**: 0.4402 (was 0.5539). 

The confidence interval is now tight [0.432, 0.449], confirming this is a genuine drop rather than noise. Overall 9-gen F1 barely budged at 0.7510 versus 0.7516, showing Gemma's minimal impact on the aggregate. Most other generators stayed stable within ±1 percentage point, though Mistral dipped slightly more at -1.1 pp. My earlier assumption that small sample size was driving Gemma's poor performance turns out to be wrong — even with full data, the classifier still can't reliably identify Gemma gen-1.

What's emerging is that Gemma-2-2B genuinely lacks distinguishable depth-1 patterns: it nails gen-0 (0.991) and gen-2+ (0.856) but bottoms out on gen-1 (0.036), pushing the binary F1 below chance at 0.440. This is actually a meaningful finding — some models may just not produce separable intermediate-generation signatures. The old results were indeed unreliable due to sample size, which the narrowed CI confirms, but this new result holds up. That said, calling this a "success" feels like a stretch when one generator is hitting near-zero F1 on gen-1 and failing the binary classification entirely. I'm also noticing the gen-1 F1 shifted from 0.0625 down to 0.0363 between the old and new runs.

So what I'm taking away: the data fix is working and the confidence intervals have tightened dramatically, which validates my earlier concern about incompleteness. Gemma's weakness is real, not a data artifact. The gen-1 signal truly is that weak even with proper data. The 9-gen model stays stable across runs, but I still haven't resolved that tail_decay inconsistency.
```

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
Now I have the full rerun results. Key findings:

1. **Gemma gen-1 F1 = 0.0363** with full data (4,957 rows). CI is now tight [0.622, 0.634] for 3-class macro. This confirms: **Gemma's weak depth-1 signal is REAL, not a data artifact.** The classifier can separate gen-0 (0.991) and gen-2+ (0.856) but not gen-1 (0.036).

2. **Gemma binary F1 = 0.440** [0.432, 0.449] — below chance (0.5), tight CI. 

The classifier performs worse than random when trying to distinguish gen-1 from gen-2+ in Gemma text.

3. **Overall 9-gen**: 3-class=0.751, binary=0.666. Delta vs 8-gen: -1.9 pp.

4. **Other generators stable**: max delta for original generators is Mistral at -1.1 pp.

5. **tail_decay inconsistency still noted** in results JSON.

The question is whether Gemma should be included or excluded from the 9-gen evaluation. Scientifically, Gemma's gen-0 (0.991) and gen-2+ (0.856) show strong separation, but the gen-1 collapse creates a real problem for the overall model performance.

However, this gen-1 weakness is actually revealing something important: Gemma's first-generation text appears to be indistinguishable from either human or deeper-generation text, suggesting some models may have abrupt rather than gradual depth transitions. For the paper, including Gemma in the 9-gen LOGO evaluation is defensible if I properly contextualize this as a model-specific phenomenon. The overall 9-gen F1 of 0.751 still meets the threshold, so I'll proceed with posting the review.
```
